there is no data source.\"\n\n        if args.offload:\n            ray.get(actor_model.async_offload())\n            ray.get(rollout_manager.async_onload(tags=[GPU_MEMORY_TYPE_WEIGHTS]))\n\n        ray.get(actor_model.async_update_weights())\n\n        if args.offload:\n            ray.get(rollout_manager.async_onload(tags=[GPU_MEMORY_TYPE_KV_CACHE]))\n\n        if args.eval_interval is not None and (\n            (rollout_id + 1) % args.eval_interval == 0\n            or (num_rollout_per_epoch is not None and (rollout_id + 1) % num_rollout_per_epoch == 0)\n        ):\n            ray.get(rollout_manager.async_eval(rollout_id))\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    args = parse_args()\n    train(args)\n","numLines":98,"startLine":1,"totalLines":98}}}

Check constraints -------------------------------------\n        satisfied_count = 0\n        for pos, student in enumerate(positions):\n            left_neighbour = positions[(pos - 1) % N]\n            right_neighbour = positions[(pos + 1) % N]\n            expected = set(desired[student])\n            actual = {left_neighbour, right_neighbour}\n            if actual == expected:\n                satisfied_count += 1\n\n        if satisfied_count < N:\n            # Partial credit: fraction of satisfied students.\n            fraction = satisfied_count / N\n            score = -1.0 + 2.0 * fraction\n            # Never reach +1.0 unless perfect.\n            return min(score, 0.999)\n\n        # --- 5. Cost-based scoring (all constraints satisfied) --------\n        if total_cost <= optimal_cost:\n            return 1.0\n\n        # Valid but suboptimal.\n        # excess = (actual - optimal) / max(1, optimal+1)\n        # score  = 1 - 2 * excess, clamped to [-1.0, 1.0].\n        excess = (total_cost - optimal_cost) / max(1.0, float(optimal_cost) + 1.0)\n        score = 1.0 - 2.0 * excess\n        return max(-1.0, score)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Helpers\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def _extract_answer(self, output: str) -> Optional[str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the text between the configured answer markers.\"\"\"\n        left, right = self.answer_markers\n        pattern = re.escape(left) + r\"(.*?)\" + re.escape(right)\n        matches = list(re.finditer(pattern, output, re.DOTALL))\n        if matches:\n            return matches[-1].group(1)\n        return None\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _parse_operations_from_text(text: str) -> Optional[List[List[int]]]:\n        \"\"\"Aggressive fallback parse: find lines that look like cycles.\n\n        Only called when no answer-marker tag was found.  Returns ``None``\n        when the text is empty/whitespace or when no integer cycles can be\n        extracted – the model did not follow the required format in a\n        recoverable way.\n        \"\"\"\n        if text.strip() == \"\":\n            return None\n\n        text = re.sub(r\"```[a-zA-Z]*\\s*\", \"\", text)\n        ops: List[List[int]] = []\n        for line in text.splitlines():\n            line = line.strip()\n            if not line:\n                continue\n            parts = line.split()\n            try:\n                op = [int(p) for p in parts]\n            except ValueError:\n                continue\n            if len(op) >= 2:\n                ops.append(op)\n\n        if not ops:\n            return None  # no cycles found → unparseable\n        return ops\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _cycle_cost(perm: List[int]) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Sum of lengths of non-trivial cycles in *perm* (perm[i] = image of i).\"\"\"\n        n = len(perm)\n        visited = [False] * n\n        total = 0\n        for i in range(n):\n            if not visited[i]:\n                length = 0\n                j = i\n                while not visited[j]:\n                    visited[j] = True\n                    length += 1\n                    j = perm[j]\n                if length > 1:\n                    total += length\n        return total\n\n    @classmethod\n    def _best_cost_for_circle(\n        cls, target_order: List[int]\n    ) -> Tuple[int, List[int]]:\n        \"\"\"Find the minimum cycle-decomposition cost over all rotations and\n        reversal of *target_order*.  Returns ``(best_cost, best_perm)`` where\n        *best_perm* is the position→student mapping that achieves *best_cost*.\n        \"\"\"\n        n = len(target_order)\n        best_cost = 10 ** 9\n        best_perm: List[int] = []\n\n        # All rotations of the forward order.\n        for shift in range(n):\n            perm = [target_order[(pos + shift) % n] for pos in range(n)]\n            c = cls._cycle_cost(perm)\n            if c < best_cost:\n                best_cost = c\n                best_perm = perm\n\n        # All rotations of the reversed order.\n        rev_order = list(reversed(target_order))\n        for shift in range(n):\n            perm = [rev_order[(pos + shift) % n] for pos in range(n)]\n            c = cls._cycle_cost(perm)\n            if c < best_cost:\n                best_cost = c\n                best_perm = perm\n\n        return best_cost, best_perm\n","numLines":308,"startLine":1,"totalLines":308}}}

Create a graph for finding neighbors\\n    graph = defaultdict(list)\\n    for i in range(4):\\n        neighbors = [circle[(i + (i % 3) % 4) % 4] for i in range(4)]\\n        graph[i] = neighbors\\n    \\n    # The K lines should each describe one operation: a space-separated list of the students involved in that operation, in the order `(b_1, b_2, ..., b_m)`.\\nExample (do **NOT** include the backticks or quotes):\\n```\\n0 1 2\\n1 2\\n2 3\\n```\\nThis means:\\n- There are 3 operations,\\n- The first operation rotates students 0 → 1 → 2 → 0,\\n- The second rotates (swaps) students 1 ↔ 2,\\n- The third rotates (swaps) students 2 ↔ 3,\\n- And the total cost is `3 + 2 + 2 = 7`.\\n<|im_end|>\\n<|im_start|>assistant\\n```python\\nfrom collections import defaultdict\\n\\ndef rearrange_students(circle):\\n    \"\"\"\\n    Rearranges the students in a circle such that every student is adjacent to both of their desired neighbours,\\n    given a list of circular permutations.\\n    \\n    :param circle: List of students (0, 1, 2, 3) circularly arranged.\\n    :return: A list of operations that achieve the desired circular arrangement.\\n    \"\"\"\\n    # Create a graph for finding neighbors\\n    graph = defaultdict(list)\\n    for i in range(4):\\n        neighbors = [circle[(i + (i % 3) % 4) % 4] for i in range(4)]\\n        graph[i] = neighbors\\n    \\n    # Force all operations to move the outer circle\\n    for action in rotate(circle):\\n        out_degrees = [graph[student][1] for student in action]\\n        nearest_one = out_degrees.index(min(out_degrees))\\n        if nearest_one == 1 or nearest_one == 3:  # don\\'t twice adjust 2 or 3\\n            shift = nearest_one\\n        else:  # else neighbors of nearest one are outpates next to 2 or 3\\n            neighbors = graph[nearest_one][::-1]\\n            if len(neighbors) > 2:\\n                shift = neighbors.index(2) if shift == 2 else neighbors.index(3)\\n            shift += 1\\n        graph[shift][1] = circle[:shift][1]  # Stretch to the right size for removal from cache\\n        tmp = [friend for student, friend in zip(shift, circle[shift] + list(shift))]\\n        neighbor_distribution[shift].extend(tmp)  # Distribute remaining students to neighbor\\n    \\n    # Reverse the graph and rotate the circle back in place\\n    graph = {index: word for word, index in graph.items()}\\n    for index in range(4):\\n        circle[circle.index(circle[index])] = graph[index]\\n    shift = list(graph).index(0) + 1\\n    while shift:\\n        while shift:\\n            circle[shift - 1] = force_to_index(circle[shift - 1])\\n            shift -= 1\\n        shift = circle.index(circle[shift]) + 1\\n    minimize_cost(shift)\\n    return shift\\n    \\ndef rotate(circle):\\n    \"\"\"\\n    Performs a circular shift of all students in the circle.\\n    :param circle: List of students (0, 1, 2, 3) circularly arranged.\\n    :return: List of circular permutations (students, after shift).\\n    \"\"\"\\n    return cartesian_product([circle], [const])\\n\\ndef force_to_index(student):\\n    \"\"\"\\n    Converts a circular index to a regular index.   (start + 1, stop - 1) if start > stop else (start + 2, stop - 2)\\n        )\\n        if slots[0] > slots[1] or slots[0] == 3:\\n            start = 3\\n            slots = slots[slots[1] : try:\\n                slots[start + 1].popleft()\\n                slots[start + 2].popleft()\\n            except IndexError:\\n                unchanged = True\\n        slots = slots[:start + 1] + slots[1:end]\\n        changed |= changed\\n        slots = [x[3:] for x in slots]\\n    return changed\\n        \\nprint(swapNeighbors([0, 1, 2]))\\n```\\n\\nRuntime tests show this solution does an
